Always consult with your doctor or physical therapist for personalized guidance and clearance before starting any exercises after a wrist fracture.\n\n**Phase 1: Early Stage (Immobilization)**\n\n* **Finger Flexion and Extension:** While your wrist is immobilized, gently bend and straighten each finger individually. This helps maintain range of motion in the wrist joint. Repeat 10-15 times in each direction, several times a day.\n* **Shoulder and Elbow Exercises:** Since your wrist is immobilized, it's important to keep the surrounding joints mobile. Perform gentle shoulder shrugs, arm circles, and elbow flexion and extension exercises.\n\n**Phase 2: After Cast Removal (Initial Healing)**\n\n* **Wrist Flexion and Extension:** With your forearm supported, slowly bend your wrist up and down, as far as comfortable. Avoid forcing the movement. Repeat 10-15 times, several times a day.\n* **Wrist Radial and Ulnar Deviation:** With your forearm supported, gently move your hand towards your thumb (radial deviation) and then towards your pinky finger (ulnar deviation). Repeat 10-15 times in each direction, several times a day.\n* **Grip Strengthening:** Use a soft stress ball or putty to gently squeeze and release. Start with a few repetitions and gradually increase as your strength improves.\n* **Forearm Pronation and Supination:** With your elbow bent at 90 degrees, rotate your forearm so your palm faces up (supination) and then down (pronation). Repeat 10-15 times in each direction, several times a day.\n\n**Phase 3: Advanced Rehabilitation (Regaining Strength and Function)**\n\n* **Resistance Band Exercises:** Use a light resistance band to perform wrist flexion, extension, radial and ulnar deviation exercises. Gradually increase the resistance as your strength improves.\n* **Weight-Bearing Exercises:** Start with light weights (1-2 pounds) and gradually increase the weight as tolerated. Perform wrist curls, reverse wrist curls, and grip strengthening exercises.\n* **Functional Activities:** Gradually incorporate activities that use your wrist in daily life, such as opening jars, using utensils, and typing. Start with simple tasks and gradually increase the complexity.\n\n**Important Considerations:**\n\n* **Pain Management:** Listen to your body and stop any exercise that causes pain.\n* **Gradual Progression:** Start slowly and gradually increase the intensity and duration of your exercises.\n* **Consistency:** Aim for regular exercise sessions, even if they are short.\n* **Professional Guidance:** Consult with your doctor or physical therapist for personalized guidance and to ensure you are progressing safely.\n\nRemember, healing takes time.
